--------------------------------


A 19-year-old female pilot, raised in a steampunk hard science fiction civilization 521 years after a supernova destroyed its connection with Earth, learns to feel the heartbeat of an entire world with her body, and then flies out to take care of it—because it is still growing.

---

## §1 The Senses of the World—What You Experience Standing on the Archeon

### 1.1 Sky

Archeon's primary star is a K2V main-sequence star—slightly smaller, cooler, and longer-lived than the Sun. Its light has a warm, orange-white hue. This means that Archeon's sky isn't the cool blue of Earth, but rather a warm blue with a honey-like undertone. The base of the clouds is golden. Sunrises and sunsets are tinged with deep copper and amber hues.

A day in Archeon lasts 27 hours, 3 hours longer than on Earth. Daily routines are essentially the same—working at sunrise and resting at sunset. However, dawn and dusk each last about 20 minutes longer than on Earth. These 20 minutes give Archeon a unique quality of light: a **long twilight**. The sun is already low, but the sky is still bright, and orange light shines on the copper rooftops, making the whole city seem immersed in amber. Pilots call this period the "golden hour"—good visibility, stable air currents, ideal for flying. Markets are busiest during this time.

**The Night Sky.** This is Archeon's most stunning visual element. 521 years ago, the supernova Betelgeuse exploded. When its light reached Archeon, its remnants formed a massive emission nebula—**Betelgeuse Nebula (Sky Fire's Remnant)**—spanning 15-25 degrees across Archeon's dome (approximately the width of 30-50 full moons). It's not a point of light, but a **river of light**. Its colors: an outer shell of violet and blue-green oxygen emission, a core of deep red and rose-colored hydrogen emission filaments, and a transition zone of orange and amber light. It emerges from the horizon 30-40 minutes after dark, slowly traversing the sky over time. On moonless nights, the nebula's light is enough to cast blurred, colored shadows on the ground—purplish-gray, like moonlight in a dream.

**Two Moons.** The larger moon is ochre and slow-moving, its surface covered with a dirty, icy sheen; the smaller moon is silvery-gray, tracing a rapid arc. The nights with both moons and nebulae hanging in the sky simultaneously are the most beautiful images in the memories of the Archeon children.

**Seasons.** Archeon's orbital period is approximately 250 Earth days (about 222 Archeon days), and its rotational tilt is only 13 degrees (half the Earth's). This means that seasonal temperature differences on a global scale are mild—there are no harsh winters or scorching summers. Seasons are more like a slow breath of "warm/cool/transitional seasons" rather than distinct four seasons. What truly drives climate differences is **topographic microclimates**—young plate movements have created fractured canyons, plateaus, and basins, each creating its own microcosm. On the same day, the bottom of Atheria Valley is warm and humid, while the top of the cliffs is dry and cold. Anna, flying from one settlement to another, experiences climate changes far greater than the temperature difference between the warm and cool seasons at the same location.

An astronomical phenomenon during the cool season amplifies the psychological effect—**Winter Veil**: interstellar dust periodically obscures part of the nebula's brilliance, making the sky appear grayer and more empty. It is only when Winter Veil arrives that the "cool season" truly gains the weight of "winter."

### 1.2 Landforms—Real Geology More "Fantastic" Than Earth

Archeon's planetary age is approximately 3.5 to 4 billion years—on the same scale as Earth. However, its plate tectonics are exceptionally intense: its core has a high abundance of radioactive elements (the star system is rich in metals), and its mantle is continuously heated by bilunar tides. As a result, its surface has been constantly reshaped, far more fragmented and dramatic than Earth's.

The canyons are deeper than the Grand Canyon, with nearly vertical 400-500 meter cliffs on both sides. The precipices are steeper than the Great Rift Valley. Table mountains and pinnacles are carved into a labyrinthine network of canyons by rivers. A first-time viewer of the Aethelgard continent might think it's more like an RPG map. But it's perfectly geologically accurate—not because the planet is young, but because geological activity has never slowed down. Iceland's landscape is so angular it looks freshly cleaved, but the crust it sits on is anything but "young"—it's continuous activity that keeps it sharp. Archeon is the Iceland of an entire planet.

This topography is a core shaping force of civilization. The rugged terrain made ground transportation extremely difficult—no railways or roads could be built. This is why airships were the lifeline of this world. At the same time, canyons and plateaus created natural microclimates: every few tens of kilometers there was a different ecosystem, each community with its own unique character.

All human civilizations are concentrated on the only habitable continent, **Aethelgard**.

#### Overview of Aethelgard (Refer to the world map image within the project)

The continent's shape is elongated in the east-west direction, resembling an irregular, thick wedge.

**North and West: Azure Main Ocean.** Sea breezes bring moisture from the northwest. The northwest coast of the continent is mild, with Iris Hollow, Driftmoor, Tidemark, and Shellharbor along its coast.

**North-Central Region: Main Basin.** A low-lying temperate plain, partially surrounded by the Orun Plateau (south) and the Serpent's Tooth Mountains (east). The vast majority of the population and farmland are concentrated here. Frontier City is located in the center, while Atheria lies in a canyon on the northeastern edge of the basin.

**Southern Region: Orun Plateau.** A massive basalt plateau stretching east to west across the continent (see geological description below). It divides the continent into two worlds—the humid and habitable Basin to the north, and the arid Great Ochre Desert to the south. Orun terminates at the coast in the west, forming a spectacular cape. Its eastern end gradually merges with the terrain of Serpent's Tooth.

**Southeast: Serpent's Tooth + Undeveloped Badland.** A separate mountain system, 3000-6100m. The Kestrel Basin (home of Skyleaf) is nestled within it. To the east and south of the mountains lies undeveloped, fragmented wasteland—too extreme in terrain and too far from the Basin for anyone to have had the resources to cultivate it for 521 years.

**South of Orun: Great Ochre Desert + South Coast.** The Orun rain shadow effect creates the reddish-brown desert. The desert's color has an even older origin—over billions of years during the early stages of Archeon's formation, the slow accumulation of abiotic oxygen in the atmosphere reacted with surface iron minerals, forming thick layers of iron oxides; Archeon's oldest "rust." The rain shadow effect exposed and eroded these red rock layers, spreading debris across the entire southern lowlands. Axiom Scar is located here. Saltmarch lies at the northern edge of the desert. The desert extends for hundreds of kilometers before reaching the south coast—the specifics of the south coast are unknown.

#### Orun Plateau—Geological Composition

Orun is not a mountain range—it is a continental plate that was uplifted as a whole by a thrust fault.

**Formation Mechanism:** Hundreds of millions of years ago, during plate tectonics, the southern plate crossed over the northern plate along a low-angle thrust surface (~35°). The result was an extremely asymmetrical topography: the north side (windward/Basin side) is a 35° escarpment with a 3km vertical drop—viewed from the Basin plain as a wall cutting from the horizon to the zenith. The south side (leeward side) is a true plateau—gradually descending from 3km to 2km, with an extremely gentle slope, extending for hundreds of kilometers. **It doesn't look like a mountain because it isn't—it's a large piece of land that has been pushed up.**

**Rock Structure (Titan's Staircase):** The escarpment surface is composed of alternating layers of basalt (deep black, hard) and shale (grayish-brown/reddish-brown, softer). The basalt layer resists erosion, forming the "treads" of the steps; the shale layer weathers and recedes, forming the "rising surfaces" of the steps. Layers of black basalt steps are covered with emerald moss—black and green are the visual signature of Orun's windward side. Groundwater gushes from the basalt/shale interface, forming numerous waterfalls (Weeping Stones).

**Geological Status: Inactive.** The thrust fault has ceased sliding (possibly tens of millions of years ago). The structure is stable with no significant earthquake risk. Current landform changes are due to weathering and river erosion—the Ishkar River carved the Grand Canyon (5 km wide and 3 km deep) from the plateau over tens of millions of years, a cut through the full thickness of the hanging wall plate.

**East and West Ends:** Orun extends approximately 800 km east to west. **The western end terminates at the coastline—the thrust plate front thins here, with the escarpment height decreasing from 3 km to 800 m-1.2 km. Marine erosion has carved out stepped sea terraces, arches, and sea stacks. The sea winds of Azure Main directly impact this low wall, creating a strong and stable orographic uplift—pilots call this section the "smooth elevator." However, it is shrouded in sea fog year-round, resulting in extremely poor visibility. At the eastern end, it gradually merges with the fractured terrain of Serpent's Tooth, where erosion has created several low, broken passes.

**Climate Divide Effect:** Orun is the climatic dividing line of Aethelgard. The basin side (north) receives moisture from Azure Main, making it mild and habitable. The desert side (south) is completely dry—all moisture condenses and falls as it rises up the windward side of Orun. This rain shadow effect creates the Great Ochre Desert.

### 1.3 Sensory Snapshots of the Six Settlements

After reading this section, you should be able to "enter" each place.

---

**Frontier City** — The largest settlement, with approximately 350,000 residents, and an Apex Hub/trade hub.

What you see first is the skyline—giant CI-era domes and towers (100-300 meters) enveloped by layers of 500-year-old human growth. Vines climb the ancient nano-concrete surfaces. Orange tents stretch above the market. Grass sprouts from the cracks in the cobblestone streets. Airships are moored at different altitudes on multi-level airport platforms—the slender black lines of carbon fiber trusses alternate with the emerald green of riveted copper plates.

The sound layers are: the bottom layer is the low-frequency hum of the fusion reactor and atmospheric processor (you get used to it and can't hear it anymore; it's unsettling when it stops); the middle layer is the sound of hammers, market shouts, and crowds; the top layer is the sound of wind and airship engines.

Smell: The damp, metallic smell of steam pipe exhaust, the charcoal fire and hammering sparks from Blacksmith Street, and the spicy aroma of grilled yellow skewers and weaver's noodles topping mixed together in the market.

At dusk, a steam-powered organ—the size of an entire wall—was operated by a musician using levers and pedals, producing a full range of notes, from deep, misty horns to sharp, piercing whistles. The organ's sound mingled with the tinkling of brass bells on the wind. A signal light illuminated—green signifying "Safe, come on."

Atmosphere: **Metropolis.** Noisy, crowded, diverse, and vibrant. It has everything and all kinds of people.

---

**Atheria** — Approximately 30,000 people, Anna's hometown

It drops abruptly from the northeastern edge of the central plain, entering a wide valley carved by the northern branch of the Ishkar River. The valley is about 2 kilometers wide, with cliffs on both sides reaching heights of 400-500 meters.

**Three-tiered structure:** The valley floor is the heart of life—vast, flat fields (cultivated for wheat, yellow mustard greens, and copper leaf vegetables), birched by the northern branch of the Ishkar River, its water shallow and gently flowing. Common houses, market squares, public baths, and ground-level workshops are present. Stone-based, timber-framed buildings do not exceed three stories. Most people live here.

The cliff face is the heart of the industry—the hard, protruding basalt layer and the weathered, retreating shale layer forming irregular natural terraces. Precision workshops are nestled in the temperature-controlled, sheltered middle section. Metal bridges span a narrow 2-kilometer valley, connecting the two sides. Wind turbines are located on the upper cliff face. Elevators (steam/water-powered) move up and down the cliff face—this is the daily rhythm of Atheria.

At the top is the gateway to the area—the main skyport sits atop the plateau face of the cliff. Large cargo airships dock here. At the bottom of the valley, a skyport tower approximately 200 meters high is under construction—scaffolding, cranes, and a half-finished carbon fiber frame—the most striking man-made structure on the valley skyline. The sounds of construction (hammering, welding, winches) are part of the daytime soundscape. Each time Anna returns, the tower has grown taller.

Soundscape: **Layered vertical sounds of daily life.** Within the cliffside community, the metallic sounds of workshops, the vibrations of pipes, and the creaking of lift winches travel along the basalt cliff face. At the valley floor, there is the amphitheater effect—the sound of wind, the babbling of the shallow Ishkar stream, the distant sounds of aggregate hum from workshops, and the construction noise of towers under construction.

Anna's home—**The Eyrie**—is nestled against the upper rock face of the canyon's outer edge, at an elevation of approximately 400 meters. From the front platform, one can see almost the entire basin.

Atmosphere: **Horizontal lifestyle + vertical industry + infrastructure under construction.** Airships occasionally fly overhead—their shadows gliding across the fields. Children look up, adults don't (it's a habit).

---

**Iris Hollow** — Approximately 120,000 people, one of the Apex Hubs

Coastal hill settlement. With no cliffs to cling to, the airport utilizes the hillsides as a terrace, creating a more horizontal and spread-out feel. Small airships are anchored directly to ground-level stakes on the grassy slopes, like a herd of giant buffalo grazing in the meadow. The sea breeze carries a salty scent. The surrounding woodlands are abundant, and the buildings use more wood, giving it a more pastoral and open feel than Frontier. It also houses a fusion reactor—another industrial hub. The wheat fields are golden and full at the end of the warm season; harvest season is the busiest time of the year.

---

**Amber Reach** — Approximately 50,000 people, granary of the southeastern highlands.

Aethelgard is a highland region on the southeastern coast of the continent. The central plain extends southeastward, rising to a gently sloping hilly plateau—200-400 meters above sea level—before reaching the coastline, large enough to accommodate the largest continuous farmland on the continent. Open to the ocean to the southeast, the sea breezes, having lost most of their salt content but retaining moisture, sweep over the hills: precipitation here is 30-40% higher than in the central plain, making it Aethelgard's most reliable rainfall area. The flight from Frontier City takes about one and a half to two days, and its direction does not overlap with flights to Atheria (northeast) or Riverbend (south)—it's a separate southeastern route.

**Visual Identity:** Horizontal, golden, and constantly whistling with the wind. There's no verticality like a canyon, no massive skyline like Frontier City's CI. Buildings spread along gentle hillsides—three- or four-story timber and stone structures with copper-colored roofs climb from the lower levels to the distant ridges. Wind turbines line the highest ridges, their blades spinning at a near-constant speed in the steady sea breeze—unlike the turbines on the cliffs of Atheria, tormented by canyon turbulence, the wind here is "clean," and the turbines turn quietly and evenly. In the town center stands a CI-era seed vault/grain processing facility, its copper dome gleaming a dark green in the sunlight. Now converted into a mill and granary complex, the low-frequency vibrations of the stone mills inside provide the daytime backdrop. Golden wheat fields and green pastures interweave among the buildings, and the fog line above the coast can be faintly seen to the southeast.

**Energy: CFU (Tier 1).** A small catalytic fusion unit, equivalent to Atheria. Provides baseload electricity, industrial steam, and heat for large-scale grain drying—it would be impossible to process thousands of tons of wet grain within weeks during the harvest season without a stable heat source. Field irrigation and wind towers use Tier 2 distributed energy.

**Economic Identity:** Archeon's breadbasket. Reliable rainfall + vast tracts of gently sloping arable land + CFU-driven food processing capacity—these three factors combined allow it to produce a food surplus far exceeding its own needs each year. Core exports: flour and compressed cereal (the primary source of hauler standard rations), dried straw (building materials and fuel), and high-quality seeds (the seed screening system from the CI food processing facility's heritage, the only one still operational in Archeon). Its negotiating position within Loom isn't as "hard" as a fusion reactor, but it's more widespread than Skyleaf—every settlement eats bread. A year without food would cause famine throughout Loom.

**Local Specialty: Wind Bread.** This sourdough bread is naturally fermented using trace amounts of salt carried by the southeastern sea breeze. It has an extremely hard and crisp crust, and a soft, slightly salty interior. The taste differs between bakeries facing the sea and those facing away from the sea—they use different yeast cultures. At the Thread Festival, boxes of wind bread brought by Amber Reach's delegation sold out within three days.

**Population Distribution: Scattered + Core Area.** The town center has approximately 15,000-20,000 people (around the CI granary/CFU/square/skyport), while the remaining 30,000-35,000 are distributed across a dozen or so farm settlements within a 20-30 km radius, each with 200-2,000 people, each with its own small wind tower and granary, connected to the town center by dirt roads and short skiffs.

**Soundscape: Wind + Grain + Birds.** The constant sea breeze creates multiple layers of white noise in your ears, in the grass, and on the turbine blades. During harvest season, the sounds of reaping, threshing, and milling add to the noise. In the evenings outside of harvest season, during the few seconds the wind pauses, you can hear the calls of grey-necked finches in the stubble fields—that silence itself is a sound.

**Atmosphere:** Abundant, open, and the quietude of a farmer. The people here are more reserved, more attuned to the weather, and more attentive to the changing colors of the soil than those in Frontier City. The town center is the venue for markets and festivals; daily life unfolds in the fields.

---

**Riverbend** — Approximately 8,000 people

Located inside the Grand Canyon on the Orun Plateau, the main stream of the Ishkar River forms a large bend here. The Grand Canyon is about 5 kilometers wide and 3 kilometers deep. The Riverbend is built on the alluvial terraces on the inside of the river bend.

**Riverbend's sound identity is water.** It's not the bass of a single distant river, but a capillary network of water sounds permeating the entire town—cliff springs have been transformed into a network of artificial channels, flowing alongside you on every level of town. Near field: the sound of water flowing in the channels at your feet, overhead, and in the walls, with a different rhythm at each corner. Mid field: small waterfalls cascading into the Ishkar River from various terraces. Far field: the broad, low-frequency, continuous breathing of the main stream of the Ishkar River. Human voices are enveloped by the sound of water—the people of Riverbend's attitude towards the sound of water is like that of people at the seaside towards the sound of waves: their silence is unsettling.

The terraced cliffside architecture features a canal system that not only supplies water and generates electricity (with miniature waterwheels at each drop level), but also facilitates logistics – goods are placed in waterproof containers, lowered into the upper canal entrance, and propelled by the water flow to the lower levels. Children use small wooden boats to "deliver messages" in the canals. On hot days, residents chill fruit in the canals.

Economic Status: **Smoked River-Runner**. Fish from the Ishkar River spawn upstream during a specific season, passing through river bends—the fishing season lasts 2-3 weeks. The fish are salted and then slowly smoked for three days with Sentinel Pine sawdust. Smoking racks are arranged in rows along the riverbank, the light smoke drawn into a thin veil by the canyon air currents, and the amber-colored aroma of wood smoke permeates the entire riverbed. Smoked River-Runner is widely recognized as a top-tier ingredient in Archeon.

Unique timekeeping feature: **Shadow Clock** - The canyon is 5 kilometers wide, and the shadow on the opposite cliff moves with the angle of sunlight. The time is determined by reading the shadow position on the ground markings.

Temperament: **Practical, serene, and with its own rhythm.** Riverbend's version of the Sky Fire legend is the most pragmatic—"Sky Fire is a physical phenomenon, not a story. But we survived—that part can be a story."

---

**Skyleaf Basin** — Approximately 5,000 people

A high-altitude basin with active geothermal activity. This settlement was built on the site of a climate monitoring/medicinal herb cultivation experimental station from the CI era. Its core product is **Ember Crown**—a deep red medicinal plant that thrives under the basin's unique geothermal, altitude, and sunlight conditions. Its deep red petals make the entire hillside appear ablaze when it blooms in the warmer season.

Ember Crown extract is Archeon's closest thing to a "panacea"—not really a panacea, but rather something else entirely. Its irreplaceability is highest in The Loom—food can be grown in many places, iron can be smelted in many places, but pharmaceutical-grade Ember Crown can only be produced in Skyleaf.

Temperament: **Remote, quiet, and feeling needed.**

---



**Energy Tiers:** Frontier City and Iris Hollow each possess D-D fusion reactors (Tier 0, non-replicable). Amber Reach and Atheria each possess CFU catalytic fusion devices (Tier 1). The remaining settlements rely on Tier 2-3 distributed energy resources (wind, solar, and hydropower).

---

### 1.4 Ecology—Resilient Earth Life Transformed

CI's Terraform project introduced genetically modified Earth organisms. Sky Fire erupted before the process was complete, leaving behind a simplified yet extremely resilient ecosystem.

#### 1.4a Archeon Atmospheric Origin and Terraform Timeline

**Archeon's non-biological oxygen—why a lifeless planet has a breathable atmosphere:**

Archeon's atmospheric O₂ baseline is approximately 18.5%. This figure has sparked decades of debate in the Federation astronomy community—almost all free oxygen on Earth is a product of photosynthesis, and a biosphere-less planet should not have this concentration.

The cause is a well-modeled, non-biological pathway: Archeon's K2V host star was much brighter in its pre-main sequence phase (300-500 million years after formation). At that time, Archeon was baked into a runaway greenhouse state, with water vapor spreading into the upper atmosphere. Strong UV radiation continuously photolyzed H₂O—the lighter H₂ escaped into space, while the heavier O remained. After the star entered the stable main sequence and dimmed, Archeon cooled, and the remaining water vapor condensed into oceans (the precursor to Azure Main). However, hydrogen was irreversibly lost, and the excess oxygen became a permanent legacy.

Over the next billions of years, free oxygen was first consumed by reducing minerals on the Earth's surface—iron reacts with oxygen to form iron oxides, which is the true origin of the reddish-brown strata of the Great Ochre Desert and the oldest "rust" on Archeon. As the mineral pool gradually became saturated (all oxidizable substances were oxidized), O₂ began to accumulate in the atmosphere. After approximately 3.5 billion years, it reached a near-equilibrium level of 18.5%.

**The Real Reason for the SSR Rating:** Archeon was designated SSR (Secondary Survey Required) not only because of its 0.98g, intact magnetic field, and liquid water reserves—but because its atmospheric spectrum showed 18.5% O₂ without any biomarker gases (such as methane). This was virtually impossible within the theoretical framework of the time. Either the atmospheric model was wrong, or there were undetectable life forms, or this was the most extreme case of abiotic oxygen accumulation on record. Either answer warranted a CI. Upon arrival, the CI confirmed: no life, purely physicochemical cause. Then it switched to terraform mode. This "anomaly" granted Archeon scientific prestige beyond its location within the Federation's colonial politics—a 520 light-year-old marginal project, yet a mecca for astrochemistry.

**Archeon in its original state (before CI arrival):** The atmosphere is predominantly N₂ and O₂, with O₂ accounting for approximately 18.5% and CO₂ present in trace amounts. There is a liquid water ocean on the surface (Azure Main), but ice sheets cover the polar and high-latitude regions, limiting open water areas. Surface temperatures are low (K2V stellar radiation is approximately 60% of that of the Sun, coupled with a low CO₂ greenhouse effect). There is no biosphere. The surface is bare rock. Advantages: 0.98g, intact magnetic field, liquid water reserves, active plate tectonics, **breathable atmosphere ready** = SSR class.

**Phase A: Warming and Water Cycle Initiation (~2470-2478, approximately 8 years)** After CI lands, the first priority is not oxygen production—oxygen is already there—but **warming**. **Polar Injection Wells** will be drilled in the polar regions to release precisely proportioned super greenhouse gases (SF₆ group + N₂O) to raise global average temperature. Within approximately 5 years, the polar ice cap will melt → Azure Main will transform from a semi-frozen area to an open ocean → the global water cycle will begin. Simultaneously, **atmospheric treatment towers** (later known as "chunky silhouettes on the ridges" during the Atwood era) will be deployed in the Basen Seven and Aethelgard habitable zones: not for oxygen production, but for **fine-tuning atmospheric composition**—scrubbing excess CO₂, adjusting the N₂/O₂ ratio, and controlling greenhouse gas concentrations to within safe limits. By the end of Phase A: global average temperature will be within the habitable range, O₂ will stabilize at ~18.5-19%, and the water cycle will be operational.

**Phase B: Bioseeding (~2478-2490, approximately 12 years).** Oxygen does not equal ecology. Bare rock has no soil—this is the real bottleneck. Strict deployment order is crucial—one wrong step and the entire food chain collapses. First wave (~2478-2480): Soil-improving microbes + earthworms + mosses and lichens. Without them, rocks will never become soil. Second wave (~2480-2485): K2V-adapted herbs + pioneer tree species (see spectral adaptation description below). Deployment areas are concentrated in the western and central parts of Aethelgard—CI conducted a triage, concentrating efforts to ensure habitability in one area; Serpent Belt and Badland are not prioritized. Third wave (~2485-2490): Crops, bee colonies, animal embryos, and songbirds. Climbing plants are deployed in built-up areas.

**Phase C: Human readiness + ecological stability (~2490-2506).** O₂ maintained at 19% (planetary baseline + minor contribution from vegetation photosynthesis). Embryos activated, early awakened individuals arrive. Basin Seven largely ready. Vegetation status upon Atwood's arrival (~2506): Patches of 25-30 year old vibratoforests on the plains (appearing like 50-80 year old mature forests due to accelerated growth genes), but far from continuous forests—patches with large areas of shrubland and bare soil between them. Farmland is productive. Habitable zone vegetation cover approximately 25%. Air is fresh and pleasant within the treatment tower area; outside the treatment tower area, it is chemically breathable but lacks the "living scent" of vegetation—no pollen, no earthy humus, smells like clean stone.

#### 1.4b K2V Spectral Adaptation—Why Archeon's Plants Aren't the Greenery of Earth

K2V stars have a spectrum peak that is biased towards orange-red (~500-600nm), and their blue and UV outputs are about 40% lower than those of the Sun. Earth's photosynthesis mainly utilizes red light (~680nm, which is plentiful) and blue light (~450nm, which is insufficient).

**CI's Genetic Modification Strategy:**
- **Extended Photosynthetic Pigment Absorption Spectrum:** An additional carotenoid-derived accessory pigment is added to the chlorophyll a/b base, absorbing the green-yellow light band of 500-580nm (K2V's strongest output band). Effect: Leaf color is not pure green—**dark green leaning towards olive**, with some species exhibiting a dark green or bronze hue. It appears rich and textured under K2V warm orange light.
**Enhanced UV Protection:** An extra layer of flavonoids/anthocyanins on epidermal cells. Side effect: Many plants develop a pale purple or reddish-brown hue on their stems and leaf veins—especially high-altitude species.
- **Accelerated Growth Switch**: CI needs decades to establish vegetation cover over the land. The first generation contains the "Accelerated Growth Switch"—a vegetative growth rate 3-5 times faster than the original Earth species. This rate weakens after several generations due to genetic drift (by design).
- **Visual Signature:** Harvest wheat fields aren't golden on Earth—they're more of a **copper/amber** color (due to secondary pigments that show color as the wheat withers). The overall world has a warm tone: honey-blue skies + deep olive-green vegetation + ochre-red deserts + copper-colored sunsets. The warm, rich color atmosphere in the concept art isn't stylized—it's a physical inevitability of the K2V star.

#### 1.4c Vegetation Zoning (Year 521 Post-Sky Fire / Anna Era)

**Zone 1: Main Habitable Zone (within the blue circle on the world map).** Extending from Iris Hollow to Kestrel's Gate, approximately 800 x 500 km. The 521-year-old vibratoforest has evolved into a **continuous woodland**—a mosaic landscape of forest, farmland, and grassland. A dense network of irrigation canals exists. Geometric plots of farmland are separated by silver vibratoforest windbreaks. O₂ approximately 20-20.5% (18.5% of the planetary abiotic baseline + 521-year net contribution of vegetation photosynthesis). The boundary of this zone equals the boundary reached by human intervention over 521 years—each generation pushing tree and grass species outwards, planting trees wherever irrigation canals extend.

**Zone 2: Orun Windward Side** The highest vegetation density in the entire Archeon region—due to continuous orographic rainfall, groundwater seepage, and a soil layer accumulated over 530 years of moss. Low-altitude Sentinel Pine forests, mid-altitude cloud belts with moss and shrubs, and high-altitude lichens. This is the location of EP13's "Windward Wonderland"—black basalt steps, emerald moss, silver waterfalls, and a sea of ​​clouds.

**Zone 3: Orun plateau surface + leeward side.** Sparse alpine meadows + drought-resistant shrubs. Strong winds, intense sunshine, and low rainfall. Transitions south to the Great Ochre Desert (completely devoid of artificial vegetation, a reddish-brown iron oxide desert). Axiom Scar is located here. Saltmarch survives on salt marshes.

**Zone 4: Serpent Belt + Undeveloped Badland.** No vegetation was introduced by the CI. 530 years of natural dispersal (wind-carrying spores and seeds) has resulted in sparse wild communities in the low-altitude valleys. The Kestrel Basin is an exception—geothermal microclimate + CI test site ruins, Ember Crown naturally established community. Badland is almost entirely coverless. Future narrative potential: the next frontier after population growth to millions.

**Zone 5: Ocean + Islands.** Azure Main phytoplankton has established a stable population over 521 years, contributing approximately 30-40% of the incremental global O₂ (above the abiotic baseline) to biogenic O₂ – the remainder comes from terrestrial vegetation. The marine food chain is extremely simple (phytoplankton → zooplankton → small fish, no large marine vertebrates). Keld Archipelago and Duskreach are unexplored. Vorhan-labeled BARREN – CI has never been touched.

**plant:**
**Quiver-Leaf Forest:** A grove of clones of aspen trees, their silvery bark shimmering in the sunlight. They tremble and rustle continuously in the slightest breeze. The sound of a Quiver-Leaf Forest in the wind is like the whisper of the entire plain. A landmark of the Central Plains.
**Sentinel Pine:** Fire-resistant bark, tenacious root system, a solitary deep green guardian standing against the wind. A silhouette against highlands and canyon walls. Sentinel Pine sawdust is a key ingredient in smoked fish.
**Sun-Berry:** Small, fingertip-sized, deep orange, and incredibly sweet berries growing high in the sun-drenched bushes on the rock face. They only bear fruit for a few weeks each year. Anna and her mother Miriam's annual harvesting ceremony—one of Anna's most cherished childhood memories.

**Animals—Selection Logic and Geographical Distribution of CI:**

CI has a limited embryo load. Each species slot undergoes rigorous utility/footprint optimization. Selection criteria: versatility (providing at least two of the following: protein, fiber, animal power, pollination, and soil improvement), low feed competition (not competing with humans for food), low genetic bottleneck risk (able to establish a viable population from a small number of founding individuals), and high environmental adaptability.

**Absolute Exclusions:** Any large carnivores, parasites, blood-sucking insects like mosquitoes and flies, or highly invasive rodents. CI does not gamble on ecological risks—a colony with only a hundred or so people cannot withstand a single rodent infestation or epidemic.

The result: Archeon's biosphere is extremely "clean"—no mosquitoes, no flies (pollination is entirely done by bees), no rats, and no snakes. No insect repellent is needed for nighttime camping. What sounds like paradise to Earthlings is simply "everyday life" to the people of Archeon.

**Core species (distributed throughout Archeon):**

**Thorn-goat:** A genetically modified goat species. Their digestive system is adapted to the local coarse vegetation; they have thick skin and long, hard, calcified horns. They are known for their aggressive temperament. They are the most important source of animal power and protein. Their milk is rich and slightly salty, and can be made into hard cheese that can be stored for over a year (standard rations for long-haul airship voyages). They are raised in almost every settlement. The largest flocks are found in canyon-type settlements (Atheria, Riverbend)—the cliffside vegetation serves as natural pasture, and the sheep roam along boardwalks and cliffside paths, making them the most common animals in Atheria. In plain-type settlements, flocks are more concentrated in pasture enclosures.

**Ridge-hen:** A modified poultry breed. Short and stocky with thick legs, their wings are so vestigial they can only flap but cannot fly. Their eggs are the most common source of animal protein—far cheaper than slaughtering a sheep. Almost every household keeps a few. The rooster's call (not the crowing of an earth rooster—a short, guttural "cluck-cluck-cluck") is one of the defining sounds of the settlement's mornings. In the Atheria, roosters are everywhere on the windowsills of cliffside houses and the railings of boardwalks, occasionally fluttering down from a height onto a lower boardwalk, causing a chaotic scramble for cover.

**Bee colonies (modified bees)**: A key species for the CI's terraform—without bees, there is no pollination, and without pollination, the entire agricultural ecosystem collapses. The modified bee species has stronger radiation resistance (survival rate during Skyfire is far higher than other insects) and extremely low aggression. Bee hives are distributed in all settlements with farmland. Honey is Archeon's most precious source of sweetness—sugar is difficult to grow, and honey is the primary source of sweetness. Skyleaf Basin flower field honey, with its light color and strong floral aroma, is the best honey in all of Archeon.

- **River-runner fish**: Fish from the Ishkar River and its tributaries; smoked dried fish is the most important way to preserve protein.

- **Soil microbiome + earthworms**: The unseen but most crucial terraform heritage. Without them, the rocks of Archeon would never become arable soil. CI introduced soil-improving microbes before any other organisms.

**Geographically Specialized Species:**

**Canal-duck:** Native to Iris Hollow and plains settlements with paddy fields. Smaller than Earth ducks, greyish-brown, and quiet (not as noisy as Earth ducks). They feed on insect eggs and aquatic plants in irrigation canals and rice paddies, a natural form of biological control. Pickled duck eggs are a local specialty of Iris Hollow. They are rarely seen in the Atheria—the canyons lack sufficient open water.

- **Songbirds (2-3 species):** A few species introduced by CI with "non-direct production functions." The reason for their inclusion may be that CI calculated in its model that mental health indicators in long-term isolated small human communities are positively correlated with environmental soundscape richness. The Grey-necked Finch is the most common—ubiquitous on the Atheria cliffs, its call is high-pitched but melodious; the sights and sounds of its flocks during seasonal changes are core material for M-08. The Rock Swallow nests along the cliff face, its flight path resembling a miniature skiff. A long-legged wading bird walks in the shallow water of the valley. These three species cover most of the bird calls heard daily by the Archeon people.

**Earth animals not in Archeon (narrative implied):**

No dogs. No cats. In CI's utility functions, companion animals take priority after food production, pollination, and soil improvement. This means that the Archeans have never had a cultural concept of "keeping pets"—spiky sheep are livestock, not pets, and rock chickens are sources of eggs, not family members. If the Federation were to contact the Archeans in the future, this could be one of the smallest but most perplexing cultural differences for both sides.

There were no horses or donkeys. In the CI era, robots handled heavy transport, eliminating the need for animal power. After the robots ceased operation (~2650+), thorn-goats took over some of the carrying function (they were already adept at using the cliffside paths), but there were no actual riding animals. The Archeons relied on airships and skiffs for long-distance travel, never riding anything.

There are no cows. The feed conversion rate is too low; the feed from one cow is enough to feed ten rock chickens. CI's resource optimization doesn't allow for such a luxury. Archeon doesn't have "milk"—all dairy products come from thorn-goat.

**Direct Consequences of the Diet:** The Archeon diet was predominantly plant-based—modified wheat bread, various preparations of yellow heads, copper leaves and beans, and a variety of roots and vegetables. Animal protein accounted for about 15-20% of daily calories (eggs > cheese > dried fish > meat), far lower than the proportion on Earth during the industrial age. Killing a thorn-goat was a big deal—usually reserved for festivals or large gatherings. Daily protein came from eggs, cheese, and dried fish. This wasn't poverty—it was normalcy shaped by 500 years of resource realities.

**Extremely low genetic diversity.** Any large-scale disease outbreak is not a minor problem, but an existential crisis. Isolated mountain ranges with their own ecosystems (such as the Kestrel Basin) are invaluable genetic vaults.

---

## §2 The Operation of Civilization—How This World Lives

### 2.1 Energy Ladder

Archeon's city distribution is determined by its energy hierarchy. Four-tier system:

**Tier 0: D-D Fusion Reactor** — A CI legacy, irreplaceable, repairable only. Only two or three are operational across Archeon. The settlements with fusion reactors are the Apex Hub—Frontier City and Iris Hollow. The fusion reactor is the heart of this civilization: 24/7 baseload electricity + heavy industry + hydrogen production (airship fuel) + waste heat driving a steam thermal chain. The design is a masterpiece of the CI era's "ultra-durable" philosophy—simple, over-engineered, and capable of being disassembled and repaired with hand tools.

**Tier 1: Steam Thermal Chain + Small Catalytic Fusion Unit (CFU)** — The steam thermal chain is not a crude 19th-century boiler—it's an optimized steam system guided by 25th-century knowledge. Organic Rankine cycle (low-boiling-point working fluid, driving turbines at lower temperature differentials). Everyday thermal drives—cranes, lifting cages, hydraulic systems, factory machinery—use steam directly. Steam pipes are sealed with CI-formulated alloy gaskets, so the network can operate for 500 years. Visually: It looks like steampunk—exposed copper pipes, exhaust ports, pressure gauges—but its performance is an order of magnitude better than any steam system in history. CFUs are smaller catalytic fusion units, enabling medium-sized settlements like Atheria and Amber Reach to achieve industrial capabilities beyond purely renewable energy.

**Tier 2: Distributed Intermediate Technology** — 25th-century knowledge + Archeon replicability. Perovskite solar panels (locally coated and produced), wind turbines (natural wind tunnels in canyons), gravity-fed hydroelectric storage (natural energy storage systems based on canyon elevation differences), and hydroelectric power (Riverbend's stepped canals are a prime example).

**Tier 3: Fully Self-Produced Renewable Energy** — Small windmills, watermills, biomass. Primary energy source for small outposts.

**General trend:** Total energy output is increasing, and industrial capacity is accumulating. **Archeon is not consuming heritage—it is building new upon the foundation of heritage.

### 2.2 The Loom – Not a System, but a Relationship

There is no central government. There are no police. There are no courts. There are no prisons.

One million people are scattered across dozens of settlements on a continent. They call the social network connecting all the settlements "The Loom." Each settlement is a thread. The airship routes are the weave of warp and weft. "We're all on the same cloth" is a common saying. If a settlement has a problem, it's called "a thread has come loose." Helping to repair it is called "mending."

The loom operates on three things:

**Craftsmanship is the passport.** The answer to "Who are you?" is always "What can you do?". Can you fix fusion reactor cooling circuits? Both Apex Hubs welcome you. Can you keep a ship afloat in the turbulence of Serpent's Tooth? Every shipping route wants to hire you. Your bread is fluffy and fragrant? There's always a line in front of your stall.

Credit is money. It operates like a small-town word-of-mouth network. Pilots, caravan leaders, itinerant doctors, shuttles—these people moving between settlements—are living information networks. A person's name is their credit card, their good or bad record lasting a lifetime.

**Airships bring gossip.** There are no newspapers, no radio. All "news" is brought by people. Each airship that docks carries two things: cargo and news. This makes the arrival of each airship a small event.

Anna's work—traveling through each settlement to sense whether the rhythm was smooth—is metaphorically described by Loom as "shuttle walking." Like a shuttle moving between the warp threads, it reads the tension of the fabric. Being called a "shuttle walker" means your skills and credibility are recognized by multiple settlements.

Settlements are not hierarchical, but neighborly. Frontier City and Iris Hollow have the greatest influence because they control the fusion reactor, but this is achieved through equal negotiations of "you need my deuterium, I need your food," not through commands. Each settlement has a Council—not a formally elected body, but more like a loose collection of "a few people whose words carry weight."

Maintaining order: Daily disputes are resolved through social pressure (if you do something wrong, the whole city will know within a day, no one will trade with you, and no workshop will take you as an apprentice). Serious crimes have two levels of punishment—**Mending**: high-risk public labor, literally "making up for what you owe." **Severance**: your name is spread throughout all settlements via the airship network, and you cease to exist on the loom. There are no more than a few dozen such cases in the records of 521 years.

**Trading System:** Barter + Credit + Semi-standardized Trading Medium "Copper Points" (standard-sized small copper pieces; copper itself has practical value). Nobody hoards copper points—hoarding too many is hoarding.

### 2.3 Daily Life – The Rhythm of a Day

### 3.1 The Rhythm of a Day

Archeon's day begins with sound.

The large settlement (Frontier City, Atheria) has a tradition called the **"morning horn"**—not a horn, but steam. At 5:30 a.m., the atmospheric processor's routine exhaust valves open, emitting a deep, drawn-out whine that can be heard throughout the city. This is the signal that "a new day has begun." It wasn't designed—it was originally just a byproduct of engineering maintenance—but over the centuries it has become a tradition, so much so that if the exhaust is even five minutes late one day, half the city will anxiously look up at the sky.

The hour following the morning call is the quietest time of day for the people of Archeon. The workshops haven't started their furnaces yet, market stalls are being set up, and airport ground staff are checking the airships departing that day. The teahouse (Archeon's version of a café/pub) is the first to open—a dark brown hot drink made with locally roasted chicory root and some modified grain, which the people of Archeon call **"Black Iron**, so bitter it will send shivers down your spine, is the ritual to start the day.

The morning is the prime working time. The workshops are running at full speed, hammers clanging, steam hissing. Apprentices are doing their tasks for the day under the guidance of their masters. The market fully awakens around nine o'clock—vendors shout, customers haggle, and occasionally a burst of laughter or a curse drifts from some corner.

Lunch is the social heart of Archeon. There is no concept of "individual dining"—eating alone is considered abnormal in Archeon culture. In workshops, all apprentices and masters eat around a long table. Airport crews eat crammed into kitchenettes below deck. If you're a shuttle and happen to be passing through a settlement during lunchtime, any workshop or household will invite you to sit down—refusing someone's meal is a slight insult in Archeon (meaning you don't trust their food or don't want to build a relationship with them).

The pace slows down a bit in the afternoon. During the hot season, outdoor activities at large settlements decrease in the afternoon, and people move indoors or to shady spots. This is the busiest time for apprentices to learn theory (looking at hand-drawn blueprints and listening to their masters explain the principles), for the weaving workshop (indoor work is unaffected by the sun), and for stories/songs to be most easily heard in the teahouse.

As evening falls, the airships begin their return journey. The signal lights above the airport (a system of color-coded oil lamps) illuminate, guiding the airships into position. The hustle and bustle of unloading, maintenance, and resupply continues until nightfall. Meanwhile, the other side of the city begins to light up—the warm glow of streetlights (gas/hydrogen lamps connected to steam pipes), pub lights, and family windows.

Nighttime belongs to stories, music, and starlight. On clear nights, Sky Fire's Remnant (Betelgeuse Nebula) stretches across half the sky—a river of purple, orange, and rose-colored light, Archeon's unchanging canopy. People sit on rooftop terraces, balconies, and canyon edges, chatting, drinking canyon beer, and watching the nebula until they get sleepy and go back to bed.

### 3.2 Architecture and City

**Core Visual Principle: The skeleton of the CI era + 500 years of human skin.**

A typical Frontier City building, viewed from a distance, it is an irregular vertical form—wide and solid at the base (the industrial modular foundation left by CI, gray-white concrete/alloy), narrowing in the middle while warmer tones begin to appear (floors built by humans using local stone and recycled metal), and expanding again at the top (timbered balconies, platforms, and airship mooring arms cantilevered outwards). The overall silhouette resembles a tree—thick roots anchored in the CI heritage, with a canopy reaching towards the sky.

Vines and plants covered every surface not actively cleaned by humans. This wasn't a sign of decay—climbing plants introduced during the Terraform era were designed to be extremely resilient; their root systems actually helped stabilize aging concrete (a certain acidic substance secreted by the roots reacted with the concrete surface to form a hardened layer). Therefore, the architects of Archeon (they didn't call themselves "architects," but "stacking men") didn't remove all the vegetation, only those that might damage the structural joints. The result is the interwoven green copper look you see in Concept Art.

**Skybridges are everywhere.** In high-density settlements, almost every building above three stories is connected by a bridge. Materials range from elaborate iron trusses (main thoroughfares) to simple planks and ropes (shortcuts between neighbors for casual chats). Skybridges are more than just transportation—they are clotheslines, signal cable supports, storm drains, and temporary stalls for vendors. On a busy skybridge, people might be walking, drying laundry, a child perched on the railing watching a blimp, and an old man selling roasted sweet potatoes from a small shed in the middle of the bridge.

**Airports are the heart of a city, not its periphery.** Because airships are the only long-haul transportation option, airports aren't pushed to the city's outskirts like airports on Earth—they're directly embedded in the city's core structure. The largest CI (City of Airships) legacy towers often double as main mooring towers, with multi-level platforms surrounding them housing loading and unloading areas, maintenance docks, and refueling stations. Image 3 (Frontier City Airport) showcases this structure: massive platforms cantilever outwards, airships are moored at different altitudes, ground crew move between stairs and elevators between platforms, and below lie markets and residential areas.

The sounds of the city are also layered: the bottom layer is the sound of flowing water and the low-frequency hum of heavy machinery (fusion reactors, atmospheric processors); the middle layer is the sound of people, hammers, and the clamor of the market; the upper layer is the sound of the wind and the whooshing of airship engines. People living on different floors have different "main melodies".

### 3.3 Food Culture

521 years—enough for a civilization to develop a complete, complex, and regionally proud culinary tradition.


Flour – a modified wheat introduced by Terraform during the CI era: short-stalked, tolerant of poor soil, and high in protein. Used for bread (Archeon has a wide variety of breads, each settlement has its own signature recipe) and noodles (wide noodles made with lye are a signature dish of Frontier City).

Root vegetables—a tuberous crop combining the characteristics of potatoes and sweet potatoes, the Archeons call it **"Yellowhead"**. Extremely hardy to grow, with stable yields, and a texture somewhere between chestnuts and yams. Roasted, stewed, mashed, sliced ​​and fried—a versatile ingredient. The Atheria Canyon version of Yellowhead, irrigated with melted canyon ice water, is said to be sweeter (the Atheria insist on this, while people in other settlements roll their eyes).

Protein – the main source of daily protein is **Ridge-hen eggs** – almost every household raises a few thorn-hens, providing eggs daily. Next is thorn-goat milk and cheese. Thorn-goats are a modified goat breed introduced by Terraform, with a digestive system specialized to eat the coarse vegetation native to Archeon. They have thick skin, coarse hair, and a fierce temperament. Their milk is rich and salty, and the cheese made from it comes in two types: hard cheese (which can be stored for over a year and is standard rations for long-haul airship voyages) and soft cheese (fresh, sprinkled on warm bread). River-runner fish – fish from the Ishkar River and its tributaries, smoked and dried – are the most important way to store protein. Slaughtering goats for meat is only done for festivals and gatherings – not on a daily basis. Iris Hollow also has canal-duck, salty and savory; outsiders find it too salty, but locals have eaten it since childhood.

Vegetables—primarily modified cruciferous and legume plants. A dark leafy green called **"copper leaf"** appears at almost every meal. Various beans are used in soups or ground into powder.

**Regional Characteristics:**

**Frontier City:** As the largest settlement and trading hub, it boasts the most diverse cuisine. The signature dish is **"Weaver's Noodles"**—wide alkaline noodles topped with a topping made from diced thorn-goat meat, yellowtail chunks, copper leaves, and a fermented spicy sauce. Each noodle shop keeps its topping recipe a secret. During the Thread Festival, noodle shops across the city hold an unofficial "Best Noodles" competition, judged by the feet of the diners—the shop with the longest queue wins.

**Atheria:** The temperature-controlled caves in the canyons serve as natural fermentation chambers. **Canyon Brews**—a strong fruit spirit fermented from a local sweet and sour fruit—are Atheria's most famous export. Each valley has its own recipe, and they all dislike each other's versions. Experienced brewers can identify the valley and vintage of a spirit just by smelling it. Canyon Brews are also an important social currency in Archean society—pilots always carry several bottles from different settlements on long-haul flights, and offering them to others at the next stop is a standard way to build relationships.

**Riverbend:** Located near the fast-flowing section of the Ishkar South tributary, it produces some of the best smoked fish. Riverbend's **"smoked river fish"** is recognized as the finest ingredient in Archeon—after marinating, it's slowly smoked for three days with Sentinel Pine sawdust, resulting in a deep amber color, a slightly glossy exterior, and a translucent orange-red color when cut open. It has a salty and savory flavor with a hint of pine resinous sweetness. It's very expensive; in upscale restaurants in Frontier City, one fish can cost the equivalent of two days' wages for an average person.

**Skyleaf:** The cool climate of the high-altitude basin is ideal for a special high-protein grain (similar to quinoa). The people of Skyleaf use it to make a fermented porridge with a slightly sour taste. Outsiders often frown upon first taste, but once they get used to it, they become addicted. Skyleaf's flower field honey is another major export—light-colored and almost transparent with a strong floral aroma, it's widely recognized as the best honey in Archeon.

**Amber Reach:** **"Wind Bread"**—a sourdough bread that ferments naturally using trace amounts of salt carried by the southeast sea breeze. It has an extremely hard and crisp crust, and a soft, slightly salty interior. The taste differs between bakeries facing the sea and those facing away—the yeast community changes with the wind direction. At the Thread Festival, boxes of wind bread brought by the Amber Reach delegation sold out within three days. Furthermore, Amber Reach's seed selection system (a legacy of the CI food processing facility) makes it the sole source of high-quality seeds for all of Archeon—each settlement awaits its seed ships every planting season.

**The social function of dining:**

Let me reiterate: eating alone is not normal in Archeon. If someone eats alone for several days in a row, the neighbors will knock on the door. Not for surveillance, but out of **genuine concern**—"Are you sick? Did you have a fight? Do you need help?"

Lunch at the long table in the workshop is the most information-intensive time of day. Masters impart their unwritten lessons ("When you turn that type of valve, don't use brute force; turn it a quarter turn to the left to loosen it, then turn it to the right"). Apprentices chat about who got scolded by their master that day, while a merchant at the next table pulls a pilot aside to ask if he can bring back a box of copper leaf seeds on his next trip to Riverbend.

### 3.4 Clothing

**Basic principle: Clothes are your resume, but a happy, colorful one.**

Archeon people's daily clothing consists of three layers: a close-fitting cotton/linen shirt or vest (light-colored, usually off-white with bleach), a middle layer of functional workwear (which varies greatly depending on the occupation), and an outer layer of windproof/waterproof coat or jacket (depending on the weather and occasion).

**Colors from the Earth:** All dyes are locally sourced. Ochre/Rust Red – from iron-oxide-rich red soil from the Orun Desert region, it's the most common and cheapest dye, hence the abundance of red rooftops, tents, and scarves in Concept Art. Indigo – from a modified Polygonum species introduced by terraform, it's slightly more expensive than red and commonly seen on more formal occasions. Yellow/Gold – from Quiver-Leaf bark extract. Green – a mixture of indigo and yellow, the most labor-intensive color, reserved for those with "money and time." Black – requires multiple dyeing steps to achieve a true black, extremely wasteful of dye, and only worn on the most formal occasions.

**Professional identity is reflected in clothing:**

Mechanic/Guardian (like Anna): White shirt (oil stains are easily visible, reminding you to wash/check that the work is finished), dark red or dark brown work jacket (lots of pockets and tool loops), leather arm guards and gloves (burn and cut protection), tool belt. Anna's Guardian's Colors—white + dark red + gray—are the traditional color scheme of the Atheria Valley Guardian.

Pilots: The iconic **patched-up jacket**. Each time a new route was completed, a piece of local fabric was taken from the settlement tailor and sewn onto the jacket. Young pilots' jackets had only two or three patches, while older pilots' jackets resembled a patchwork quilt. A jacket that had flown all the routes on the network was essentially a map of Archeon, worn on the body. Add to that flight goggles (usually brass frames with polished natural mineral lenses) and a scarf (to shield against the cold winds and dust at high altitudes).

Blacksmiths/metallurgists: Thick leather aprons, thick gloves from forearms to elbows, headscarves or hair ties. Because they are constantly exposed to firelight, their clothes quickly fade to a uniform smoky gray tone, so "gray-clad men" is a nickname for blacksmiths in some settlements.

The healer: a clean blue and white color scheme, keeping things as clean as possible—this is a declaration in itself in Archeon’s dusty and greasy world ("My domain needs to be clean").

Farmers: The most practical and least concerned with color clothing. Loose, durable, and easy to move in. A large straw hat for sun protection.

**Patchwork Culture (Core Aesthetics):** Don't throw away torn clothes—pat them. But patches aren't shameful; they're badges of honor. If an apprentice's first work jacket is covered in patches and still doesn't fall apart, it means that jacket has been with them through enough work. Some people will deliberately use contrasting fabrics for patching—a bright red patch sewn onto a dark jacket, as if to say, "Look, I rubbed my shoulder in Riverbend, but I fixed it."

### 3.5 Market

Archeon doesn't have the concept of "shops"—it has **markets**.

The large settlement market is permanent, occupying ground-level areas in the heart of the city. The scene under those red and orange tents in Image 4/8/10—that's it. The stalls are semi-fixed wooden frame structures with canvas roofs, and some families have been running the same stalls in the same location for generations.

The market isn't divided into sections. Next to the bread seller might be a tinkerer, next to the tinkerer a fabric vendor, next to the fabric vendor an old man giving haircuts, and next to the hairdresser a stall selling grilled kebabs. This mix isn't a planning failure—that's the charm of the Archeon market. You go in, buy a spare part, and come out with a hot loaf of bread and three pieces of gossip about the settlement next door.

A hybrid trading system combining barter and credit: Most transactions were barter or exchange of labor for goods. You might trade a basket of fish for three bags of flour, or spend half a day repairing a blacksmith's furnace and he'd forge you a new set of pipe clamps. However, there was also a semi-standardized medium of exchange—"copper cents," small, standardized copper pieces cast by the metallurgical workshops of each Apex Hub. It wasn't legal tender (there was no "legal tender"), but because copper itself had practical value (conductivity and thermal conductivity, and could be melted down into parts) and its standardized dimensions, it was widely accepted as a medium of exchange. Nobody hoarded copper cents—hoarding too many was hoarding.

### 3.6 Music and Sound

Archeon has its own musical tradition, and it's **loud and fun**.

**Musical Instruments:**

String instruments—a variety of stringed instruments made of thorn-goat gut and quiver-leaf wood, ranging from small plucked instruments (about the size of a ukulele) to large bowed string instruments (similar to a cello). The tone is warm and slightly muted due to the properties of the wood and strings.

Percussion instruments include metal drums (made from discarded oil drums/metal cans, in different sizes and pitches), wooden drums, and a unique type of "tube bell"—copper pipes of varying lengths hung together, producing a crisp metallic tone when blown by the wind or plucked by hand. Tube bells hang on the skybridges and balconies of many settlements, making the whole city ring out on windy days.

**Steam Whistle Organ:** This is Archeon's most unique instrument. It works by using branch pipes of a steam piping system, controlling the steam flow through whistles of varying diameters via valves of different sizes to produce different pitches. The largest steam whistle organ is located in the central airport area of ​​Frontier City—a wall-sized structure operated by a dedicated musician using a row of levers and pedals, capable of producing a full range of tones, from deep foghorns to sharp whistles. During the Thread Festival, steam whistle organ performances form the background music throughout the city.

**Songs:** The Archeon people sing. Many songs. There are work songs in the workshops (rhythms that match the hammering or sawing motions), flight songs on the airships (to pass the time on long flights, often with suggestive lyrics), hawking songs in the markets (somewhere between shouting and rap), and drinking songs in the pubs (the more you drink, the louder and more off-key you become). Many songs encode practical information in their lyrics—there's a song about the various passes of Serpent's Tooth, listing the wind direction and danger level of each pass in order; flight apprentices sing this song to memorize flight routes.

---

## IV. Festivals

### 4.1 Thread Festival

Once a year, during the best flying season (usually the middle of the long summer, during the few weeks when the weather is most stable and the winds are mildest).

Each settlement sent a delegation—pilots flying decorated airships with ribbons representing their settlement's color attached to their tails—to converge on Frontier City. Frontier City's airport was the most spectacular sight in all of Archeon that week: dozens of airships of varying sizes were simultaneously anchored on platforms at different altitudes, colorful flags and ribbons fluttering in the wind, and the city's population had doubled.

**Festival Content:**

**Trade Fair:** Each settlement brings its best products for display and trade. Riverbend's smoked fish, Atheria's canyon wine, Skyleaf's alpine grains, and exquisite tools and equipment from workshops around the world. This is the largest exchange of goods of the year.

**Skills Competition:** An open competition between apprentices and masters. Events include: a standard valve assembly/disassembly speed contest, a welding precision contest, an airship simulation operation contest (using a ground simulator with wind generated by a hand-cranked blower), and a best patch sewing contest (judged by: sturdiness + aesthetics). The winner receives no material reward—the prize is **fame**, which spreads faster on the loom than any goods.

**Storytelling Event:** Storytellers from each settlement (full-time or semi-full-time oral historians/performers) take turns sharing their settlement's annual story in the central square of Frontier City. Some are serious (this year we fixed the bridge that had been broken for three years), some are funny (our Council president was chased by a thorn-goat for three blocks), and some are tear-jerking (old Kestrel died, but his apprentice independently completed the first Serpent's Tooth voyage this year). These stories are taken back by people from other settlements to be retelled, becoming part of the shared memory of the entire Archeon.

**The Marriage Market:** Not a formal arranged marriage—but Thread Festival is the best opportunity for young people from different settlements to meet. Many inter-settlement marriages originate from encounters at Thread Festival. It's not just romance—inter-settlement marriages are one of the strongest threads on the loom, establishing a blood relationship between the two settlements and making future collaborations more natural.

**Closing Night:** Steam whistles and organs played at full throttle, everyone surged into the streets and rooftops, and airships flew in formation with all their lights on—a river of light reflecting the nebula above. Children ran with lanterns, and adults drank Canyon beer and embraced each other. This was Archeon's happiest night of the year.

### 4.2 Sky Fire's Eve

The anniversary of the supernova's arrival in Archeon.

"Our birthday"**.

Daytime is like a regular holiday—no work, markets are open, and children are on holiday. Every household makes a special food—**"Sparkling Cakes"**, round baked cakes made with red dye (iron oxide red clay is a natural food coloring) and golden jam, representing the color of a supernova explosion.

As evening falls, everyone goes out to look at the sky. Sky Fire's Remnant is so large and bright in the sky that you can't help but look at it—a purple and rose-colored river of light stretches from the horizon to the zenith, Archeon's eternal canopy. On this night, people deliberately turn off the lights near them (not the whole city turns off its lights, but spontaneously—letting the light from the nebula become the main light source).

The oldest elders will tell children one version of the Sky Fire legend. **But each settlement has a different version.** Frontier City's version says, "The old furnace in the sky finally burned through." Atheria's version says, "A giant star wanted to become a river, so it shattered itself." Riverbend's version is the most pragmatic: "The Sky Fire was just the radiation shockwave from a supernova reaching our planet. It's a physical phenomenon, not a story. But we survived—that part can be the story."

Children ran around with lanterns—the lanterns were painted in the colors of a nebula: purple, orange, and rose. They compared whose lantern was the prettiest. Adults drank and chatted. Young people fell in love under the nebula's glow.
It's been 521 years. The trauma has been metabolized into part of our cultural DNA, no longer needing separate commemoration. The emotional tone of Starfire Night is **"Look how resilient/how amazing/how beautiful we are—cheers!"**

### 4.3 Launch Day (City Founding Day)

Each settlement has its own anniversary. The dates aren't necessarily precise (many are long forgotten), but rather it's more like a customary "our holiday."

**The whole city is on holiday.** The market extends until midnight. Taverns display special banners. Workshops are closed (but blacksmiths are probably busier—because they're making little metal toys for the children for the holiday).

Standard activities: Skills competitions (more relaxed and funnier than Thread Festival – there will be quirky projects such as “tightening screws with your left hand” and “identifying parts blindfolded”), music, lots of food and drinks, and Council members standing on the platform giving a short summary of “what we did this year” (if they go too long, they will be booed by the audience – this has become part of the tradition).

### 4.4 Bonfire Night – A Riverbend Special Feature

Light a bonfire at the bottom of the canyon. Sing. Drink canyon brew. Throw notes with your worries written on them (or just leaves—paper isn't cheap in Archeon) into the fire.

The tone was **joyful**. Not a gloomy campfire meditation—it was a group of people singing loudly around a fire, some dancing, some arguing (about which valley had better brewing), and a few children playing tag in the shade beside the fire.

Bonfire Night originated in the early generations after Sky Fire—back then, it was indeed about finding warmth in the darkness and fear. But the version after 521 years has completely become an excuse for **community gatherings**. If you ask a Riverbend resident, "What does Bonfire Night commemorate?", they might say, "Uh… well… the day we grilled things together?"

### 4.5 Naming Day

The child is not given an official name immediately after birth. Usually, a small gathering is held by the family and close neighbors/colleagues about a month after birth (the actual observation period to confirm the baby's health—the infant mortality rate in Archeon, while much lower than during cascade failure, is still higher than on modern Earth).

**Naming Traditions:** Names typically come from two sources—family lineage (using the name or part of the name of a deceased elder), or **trade-related** (if parents hope their child will inherit a certain skill, they will choose a name related to that skill—for example, Anna is a common name in the Atheria Valley guardian tradition). There are also names chosen purely because they "sound good."

At the nameday party, parents tie a **"first thread"** on the baby's wrist—a thin cord made of the softest thorn-goat yarn, dyed in the color of the parents' settlement. This thread will naturally wear off after a few months, but it symbolizes "you are now part of the loom."

### 2.7 Materials and Construction – What Can They Do?

**Archeon did not live by scavenging from ruins.** They were "people who built a new civilization with their own hands, standing on the foundation of 25th-century knowledge."

The five-tiered material ladder: Unreplicable CI top-tier materials (T-carbon, nano-reinforced concrete, only available in stock) → Replicable but quality-reduced advanced materials (carbon fiber composites, produced in local carbon kilns; formula alloys, formula forging from CI etching records) → Completely self-made industrial materials (carbon steel, copper alloys, masonry, wood, glass).

Carbon fiber is key—the precursor is a high-carbon fiber crop specially designed by CI. After harvesting, it is soaked, drawn, and dried into "white fibers," which are then carbonized in a kiln into "black fibers." The kiln operator knows that "firing at this temperature for so long produces good black fibers," but doesn't fully understand the chemical mechanism—a prime example of experiential engineering. When carbon fiber is combined with plant-based resin, its strength is about five times that of steel, while its weight is less than a quarter.

**Independent Construction Capability:** Standard buildings range from 3-8 stories, while large structures can reach 80-120 meters. Ultra-large structures require a CI (Computer Integrated Structure) framework. The Skyport tower under construction in Atheria and the multi-level airport platform in Frontier City are both large-scale projects in progress. Airship hoisting is the most important high-altitude construction method.

**Three layers of knowledge transmission:** CI metal plate etching records (the most authoritative technical reference) → Handwritten copies (simplified generation by generation but with added practical experience notes) → Oral transmission from master to apprentice ("Hearing this sound means the temperature has been reached"). Result: Without understanding the underlying theory, but possessing extremely strong practical engineering capabilities.

### 2.8 Airship

The airship is the lifeblood of Archeon. Buoyancy comes from hydrogen, and propulsion comes from a hybrid power system: fuel cells (hydrogen-oxygen reaction generates electricity to drive the motor, the main propulsion), steam propulsion (a small boiler directly drives the propeller, a reliable backup), and sails (auxiliary and energy-saving).

Anna's Hauler has a deep hum and a slight chemical smell characteristic of fuel cells when it starts up. Kassia's Razor is a sharper whine motor—powerful for short bursts but with limited range.

Airships are classified from Class I small private boats to Class V large cargo haulers. Anna pilots a Class II—a medium-sized vessel that balances cargo carrying and maneuverability.

### 2.9 Flying Culture – The Wind People

Archeon pilots aren't "pilots"—they're **people who talk to the wind**. The fractured terrain creates extreme microclimates: canyon venturi effects, plateau shear winds, and persistent high-altitude winds. A pilot's competence isn't "controlling the airship," but "reading the air and negotiating with it."

**Skiff Culture:** The Skiff is Archeon's motorcycle—small, open-top, and controlled entirely by the body and the wind. If a hauler is a truck, the skiff is a wing standing in the air. Almost every pilot starts with the skiff. Driving a skiff in a canyon is an experience somewhere between flying and dancing—the shifting of body weight, the fine-tuning of the arm's angle to the sail, the instantaneous perception of updrafts—all done through the body, without a dashboard, without automatic corrections.

**Thread Run (Ep25):** A skiff race takes place on a canyon route between two skyports, where pilots navigate a sequence of markers. It's not a straight dash—the route winds through wind tunnels, cliff face protrusions, and archways. Judging criteria: speed, precision (how close to the cliff face), smoothness of the flight path, and how the wind is utilized. The most dangerous person isn't the fastest—it's the one who "looks like the wind itself is flying." Spectators stand on cliffside terraces, children ride on adults' shoulders, and the entire canyon transforms into a vertical arena. Thread Run is the most anticipated event in Archeon during Thread Festival.

Two flight philosophies collide in Thread Run: Anna's "keeping pace with the wind" (perception + patience, letting the wind do the work) vs. Kassia's "being faster than the wind" (explosive power + precision, using speed to suppress uncertainty). Both styles can win—but the ways to win are different, and the aesthetics are different.

**Archeon pilots' "courage":** It's not about being "fearless"—it's about having a "more precise understanding of the airflow than it is of my power." Competence is the source of courage. They are no less afraid than anyone else, but their hands are already doing the right thing before fear arrives. This is a quality that has been selected by the environment over 500 years and 30 generations in a world without automated safety nets.

### 2.10 Supplement to Airship Fleet Parameters

**Class V Large Cargo Hauler (Archeon's Largest Airship):** Approximately 100 meters long, with a robust, flattened ellipsoidal hard shell. The effective volume of the hydrogen bladder is approximately 35,000-45,000 m³ (the bottom is a hard-shell cargo hold/engine bay/habitat). It features a carbon fiber composite frame, CI-formulated alloy reinforcement plates, and fabric skin.

The total takeoff weight is approximately 40-50 tons, with a payload of 20-30 tons and a pure cargo capacity of 12-18 tons. The design blends Federation engineering rationality (streamlined body, Nacelle layout) with Archeon's local practical aesthetics (exposed riveting, patched skin, and traces of hand-assembly). The large adjustable sail structure at the stern serves as both a stabilizing surface and a cruising sail, saving 30-40% of fuel when downwind.

A single Class V vessel can change the fate of a settlement—a single shipment can provide Riverbend with three months' worth of filters and spare parts. There are probably only a handful of Class V vessels in operation throughout Archeon.

---

## §3 Historical Skeleton—What Shaped Did This World's Past Look Like?

The goal is not to present a complete history. It's to let you know "where this civilization came from," thereby understanding "why it has become the way it is now."

### 3.1 Departure (25th Century)

Earth has not perished. ASI (Super Artificial Intelligence) has solved most material problems—energy is nearly limitless, manufacturing is fully automated, and the average lifespan is 150-200 years. However, 70% of the population has become "sleepers"—their bodies are maintained by automation, while their consciousness is immersed in a virtual world. Another 15-25% of "awakeners" choose to remain conscious, seeking meaning in physical reality.

**Note:** This scale and form are a snapshot from the 25th century.
